# Component Library Experiments And Evidence

Lifecycle: **Active evidence companion**. Owner: Gabriel Bourget. Reviewed: **2026-10-18**. [Roadmap](roadmap.md) owns current tasks, contracts, catalogue and acceptance gates. This file records bounded experiment outcomes and historical lookup; raw logs and captures stay in private evidence packets. A historical failure, source checkpoint or passing repeat retains its actual proof tier.

<a id="catalogue-acceptance-matrix"></a>

## 3. Catalogue Acceptance Matrix

This is the active M2 completion ledger for all 53 stable catalogue IDs. Gate definitions, exact scenario IDs and required platform profiles live in [the acceptance rubric](roadmap.md#31-acceptance-rubric). A cell ID is `CAT.<manifest-id>.G<n>`; native scenario cells extend it with platform/profile/scenario/case as specified there. Every mandatory capability and scenario must be instantiated and resolved before its aggregate gate can pass. An empty or uninstantiated scenario set is not vacuous success.

`OPEN` is an administrative state before reconciliation/execution; it is not a failed test. Final verification dispositions are `PASS`, `FAIL`, `BLOCKED`, `N/A(reason)` or `OWNER-DEFERRED(authority)`. Historical passes retain their recorded scope and artifacts; opening a broader aggregate cell does not invalidate those receipts or promote them to full current-matrix acceptance. Source availability is tracked in the roadmap catalogue separately. The table initially leaves unreconciled aggregates open rather than inventing complete proof from earlier test counts.

Current required native scenario projection for CircularProgress, Counter, partial Carousel, supplemental Button/Text typography, shared ClickPopover host obligations and private Tooltip/HoverPopover: [section3.31.3](#3313-private-tooltip-surfaces-and-required-native-matrix), source producer 5ffa805, preserves all 3,910 CP-44 cells and 400 old-to-current ID aliases unchanged while adding 3,396 explicit tooltip/hover obligations. All 7,306 projected installed cells remain OPEN/null with 0 native actions. This remains a supplemental projection rather than the whole 53-component matrix; other families retain their checkpoint matrices and unresolved aggregate cells. CP-51 in §3.31.29 verifies the current public consumer/graphs at 6103855 and exact public Release reuse; private Menu native acceptance remains OPEN. Private Pagination construction and every remaining native acceptance obligation stay open. The 110 activation/release installed cells and private Breadcrumbs runtime remain OPEN. The 43 new relationship obligations also remain OPEN, with one platform-specific N/A; this projection does not replace the whole 53-component matrix. The navigation/current metadata projection now has 39 scoped PASS (21 Android/18 iOS), 34 OPEN and three platform-specific N/A fields in §3.31.28. The original iOS log failure remains preserved; the new file-receipt cohort proves its exact observed forms. Later source changes require their own matching proof.

CP-68 in [section3.31.50](#33150-public-tooltip-and-hoverpopover-completion) is the current public consumer/build checkpoint at175f367. The35 earlier consumer references advance provenance, and Tooltip/HoverPopover add their public source/types/scene and consumer evidence. All private/installed/profile/closure statuses and the inherited transitive structured-clone distinction remain required OPEN.

#### 3.9.1. Installed Android Assertions And Oracle Disposition

One read-only API36/x86_64 guest is used for the whole run at 720×1600/density 280, logical app window 411×914 and fontScale 1. Tag passes actual initial state and 48-unit target, first activation, two controlled-owner vetoes, numeric 0/string "0"/empty/undefined requests, disabled suppression, programmatic selection while disabled, held-press owner-disabled takeover without a fabricated request, and false/mixed exposed-state overrides. These receipts record native hierarchy/target fields, exact owner/request text, observed touch coordinates and the matched source/APK. The partial glyph/geometry captures are not promoted to full style/profile closure.

The original Tag physical-zero oracle fails at 9.714×10.286 native units. A real browser fixture using the exact canonical theme, compatibility bridge and Tag CSS measures 10×10 for authored 0px dimensions with 5px padding under UA/default, border-box and content-box sizing; zero insets alone produce 0×0. The native result equals 17×18 physical pixels at 1.75 density, matching the padding-box minimum within one physical pixel. The original failed expectation remains raw; it is reviewed as an invalid physical-zero oracle rather than relabelled passing or repaired by changing the library. The independent zero-padding-box assertion passes, and focused scene test1dfa4d2 now separates authored zero values from observed padding-box geometry. The decimal parser's consumed sentence punctuation and an overly exact 30-unit Avatar expectation are separately retained observer defects; actual 29.714 units is 52 physical pixels, within the declared pixel-grid tolerance. #### 3.21.1. Native Inset Precedence Repair

A separate final review finds an actual owned-style defect in the newly constructed chrome. Native Button's round recipe includes paddingHorizontal7; the wrapper's padding5 or caller padding0 alone leaves that axis value intact. Pinned RN0.86.3 Yoga Style.h resolves a physical/logical edge, then Horizontal, then All, so the inherited axis value defeats the intended all-edge inset. Canonical Button CSS supplies round padding through a class and the Carousel wrapper's inline padding overrides it. The native wrapper must clear the inherited horizontal value before applying caller styles. This is separate from the earlier test-expectation corrections and is not dismissed as a mock artifact.

### 3.22. Circular Progress Semantic Label Contract Repair

The complete pinned RAC ProgressBar/React Aria useProgressBar declarations and runtime, canonical CircularProgress/helpers/Path/CSS/receipt and existing suites are rechecked for the inherited valueLabel gap. The canonical renderer never mounts valueLabel: its separate text prop owns visible SVG content. React Aria uses truthy label data unchanged as aria-valuetext and render-state valueText; falsy data uses formatted progress. React DOM serializes that attribute. #### 3.31.12. Native Activation And Release

Gabriel approves DEC.native-activation.01 on 2026-10-18. The implementation exports the third native activation event arm and its source constants, adds semantic onPressUp and preserves raw press-in/out as touch-or-keyboard interactions. Android emits from its actual click listener; iOS emits from accessibilityActivate, calls the inherited accessibility callback separately and reports the activation handled. Observation time is read at the native callback, with no fabricated touch coordinates, key codes or hardware timestamp. Both bindings retain the real native target/ref. Private monotonically increasing activation IDs reject duplicate/retired packets; hosted delivery checks native owner/window/epoch against the acknowledged scope. Native callbacks require an active attached window. Standalone queued delivery across actual window replacement/loss still requires installed proof and is not closed by these source checks.

| Rubric/matrix facet                               | Exact executable mapping in Button.activation.test.tsx                                                                                                                                                                                                               | Expected source outcome                                                                                                                            |
| ------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| 3.1.1–3.1.3 original events and semantic contract | delivers original %s activation in semantic order without raw down/up or fictitious coordinates, both source constants                                                                                                                                               | Same event object for start/up/end/action, balanced changes, zero observation time preserved, no invented fields or raw in/out.                    |
| 3.1.2 ownership/guards and current callbacks      | consumes %s activation without replay after re-enabling and uses the current callback, pending/disabled; retires held touch release during %s takeover without losing fresh native activation                                                                        | Guarded records stay consumed; held cycles cannot revive; fresh records and current callbacks work.                                                |
| 3.1.3 admission/lifetime                          | rejects foreign ownership, invalid kind/source/serial and retained callbacks after unmount; admits only the current same-window native scope session across deactivation/reactivation                                                                                | Foreign/retired/duplicate packets and inactive or old window sessions cannot act.                                                                  |
| 3.1.3 held input and release                      | finishes an admitted held pointer once and suppresses its later SDK action; ignores native activation during a held keyboard cycle and preserves genuine release order; delivers actual pointer release once with press-in delay %i                                  | No duplicate action/up; real release object and ordering survive delay ed activation.                                                              |

#### 3.31.20. Floating Native Focus Integration

The next increment starts from PR #22 head 94d79c1 on feature/native-pagination. ClickPopover's entry, modal recapture and restoration called plain View.focus even though approved §3.5.21 deliberately preserves that upstream nonportable method. The private root/dialog now use the owned focus host and actual focusNativeTarget commands. Private initial/restore targets require full native View/TextInput instances; arbitrary focus-shaped objects and browser refs fail executable type cases. The actual root ref remains unchanged in kind and layout, with no extra wrapper or method mutation. A subsequent explicit root focusable=false/zero-style regression passes in the 23-case ClickPopover suite; the three other focused suites are unchanged.

Original SDK bubbling focus/blur notifications have separate private observers, preserving original owner event identity and descendant propagation without duplicating the direct native focus acknowledgment. Observer callbacks retire with the actual host. Entry and restoration wait for native window/policy acknowledgment; queued frames capture the actual window session and cannot cross its epoch. Close restoration survives a changed restore ref, but reopen, parent/session replacement, inactive windows and unmount retire old callbacks. Native focus requests additionally refuse Android windows without focus and inactive/non-key UIKit windows/scenes. These are native foreground guards, not screen-reader focus or editor writes.

Three focused fail-before cases against the exact 94d79c1 ClickPopover source record zero native commands where the actual dialog/root/current explicit target should request one; the current source is restored after that bounded comparison. The passing focused gate covers 70 cases across ClickPopover, private Tooltip/HoverPopover composition, private Breadcrumbs and NativeFocusView. It checks actual owned refs/command arguments, default and explicit entry, current target replacement, modal recapture, nonmodal no-focus behavior, raw event identity, registered TextInput focus with controlled empty text/no writes, inactive/retired requests, restoration/ref handoff and stale window epochs. Initial restoration fixtures omitted the native policy acknowledgment after close; the corrected fixtures observe that real protocol barrier rather than relaxing production guards.

Native source types and focused lint pass. Actual Gradle owned compilation/lint passes against the pinned Android dependency graph, retaining the three existing framework warnings; the UIKit sources pass syntax compilation against matching existing codegen/SDK headers. Full native Tab order/containment/completion and current consumer/Release/installed identities remain OPEN until matching later evidence; CP-48 remains the historical 13c34cc cohort. Source test logs and compiler receipts are retained under /private/tmp/codon-native-pagination-\* for the coherent increment's later immutable packet. No XCTest/LLDB/startup investigation, guest action, pin/palette change, control-value workaround, SDK flag or signing occurs.

The complete canonical Pagination component, helper/subcomponent/CSS/label/icon source and all 30 root cases are read; the unchanged web baseline passes 30/30. Its caller-facing page/page-size contract and the local web page-size mirror conflict in one source assertion. Native construction follows Gabriel's standing requirement that controlled values remain authoritative: render and first-visible-item calculations use caller itemsPerPage, including repeated vetoes/independent echoes. Page-input draft remains distinct local state, as required by the canonical draft-across-parent-renders case. Go parses the current raw editor draft with the existing locale-aware number model and updates the distinct local draft before requesting navigation; it does not depend on a native blur event arriving first. This addresses native composition timing without changing NumberInput's public contract, writing native editor props or mirroring the caller's page/size. The original raw/Input/ComboBox failures remain untouched.

#### 3.31.23. Native Navigation And Current Page Binding

The next private adapter increment, feature/native-navigation-metadata from exact PR #23 head be75e5b, implements roadmap §3.5.27 under the existing source-owned backend and conventional-port approvals. NativeNavigation defaults false. Enabled Breadcrumbs and visible Pagination roots request real navigation-container metadata; disabled Breadcrumbs retains the canonical absence of its outer nav. Android uses the resolved NodeInfo content description as container title and a separately localizable role description. UIKit returns its real landmark container type while the flag is set, delegates otherwise and clears the flag on recycle. No extra native layout wrapper, public focus/press API, dependency, SDK flag, caller-value write or palette change is introduced.

Current-page status combines the existing native selected state with configurable accessibility value wording. Breadcrumbs' last Text stays passive and derives from the caller collection. Pagination's numeric status derives only from caller currentPage and moves only on owner echo; repeated declined requests cannot move it. Existing general value/alias text precedes the status, zero range values remain intact and dedicated empty status wording stays empty. Nested selected/expanded aliases are normalized to the owned state rather than overriding the protected semantics. The canonical source, RN role tables and actual SDK value implementation are inspected before these native equivalents are recorded.

The pinned Android SDK setter clears contentDescription when an accessibility value map is removed, even if the label prop is unchanged. Numeric Pagination buttons therefore retain an empty text map when current-page wording is removed. This preserves the SDK label recomputation path without native editor writes, caller-value mirrors or changes to generic Button. A focused transition asserts both the original name and empty status after owner echo. The original raw/Input/ComboBox failures remain untouched.

The private inspector now forwards actual platform/selected/container metadata with original event identity and existing owner/window/retirement guards. UIKit containerType is its native enum; Android containerType=-1 is explicitly unsupported. Android containerTitle/roleDescription are actual NodeInfo getter values; their empty UIKit fields are explicitly unsupported. These platform distinctions cannot be treated as native acceptance. Current passive core Text metadata still needs the platform inspection lane; no new public inspector or fabricated acknowledgment is added.

#### 3.31.35. Native TagGroup Keyboard Contract Corrections

Continue on feature/native-tag-group-input from PR #29 head 5ea80c13c7bd43fde1afbae4fce231be8042111d. The additional 22 keyboard outcomes cover all six Arrow/Home/End targets, disabled skipping/wrap, five arrow behavior/modifier forms, three distinct End modifier forms, link override versus Home/End selection, actual echoed range anchors, two RTL directions, repeated/already-complete Select All, Delete repeats and default buffered Space capture.

The oracle exposes a private native mismatch: Ctrl/Command+A must call selectAll, not toggleSelectAll. Already-all or explicit complete owners produce no clearing request; repeated vetoes still request all. Arrow movement accepts actual modifiers; Shift extends in multiple mode under both toggle and replace, and only the platform noncontiguous modifier suppresses replace selection. Home/End use Ctrl/Command+Shift for extension, ordinary Shift keeps replace semantics, and their direct manager selection can include a link even though arrow navigation with link override only focuses it. Plain caller sets do not acquire an anchor from focus: a first unanchored extension can request only the existing membership plus the endpoint. The anchored oracle echoes an actual RAC callback object rather than attaching metadata to a plain web Set; native requests preserve the same structural anchor/current information without importing web state classes. Delete repeats remain removal requests while cancelled/up/retired-window events stay guarded. Typed zero/string-zero/empty keys and caller-owned missing keys remain distinct and unchanged.

The actual pinned useTagGroup constructs its default ListKeyboardDelegate without a collator, and RAC supplies no replacement. Its useTypeSelect hook still appends a one-second text buffer and captures a following Space before item selection, while search focus has no match. The native family preserves this observed default for owned row/remove hosts using the original event, a retired timer generation and current window session. It does not invent working search, advertise a custom delegate or waive the required search capability. Complete nested/opaque capture, custom collection/delegate/context integration and the intended search equivalent remain OPEN. The one-second capture behavior is verified independently on the actual web component; source timer/Space checks are not native key-delivery evidence.

No sealed helper executes and no sealed file changes.

Roadmap §3.5.39 records the inherited focus audit and explicit native-ref/scoped Escape construction contract before implementation. Canonical React Aria Overlay restores focus, useModalOverlay enables containment, and FocusScope owns traversal/child scopes; those inherited capabilities remain mandatory. Existing Panel system dismissal does not by itself implement scoped physical Escape. This increment follows established native host/key/registry patterns, keeps Panel private and preserves all53 aggregate gate dispositions and35 public counterparts. Source progress will be recorded here with exact checks and limits; it cannot close actual focus acknowledgements, native containment/first-descendant traversal,350ms property transitions, real blur or installed/profile/closure cells.

The positive restoration checks expose a shared activation defect: NativeOverlayProvider's window predicate reads the live registry, but the application floating host does not rerender on a native dismissal alone. Its scope can remain disabled after the window returns. Subscribe the provider to its owned registry with the existing useSyncExternalStore pattern; no API, native schema, dependency or palette change is needed. Two public Modal/AlertDialog regressions assert application scope enablement before admission, disablement through presentation/closing and re-enablement after the actual iOS dismissal callback, without another owner render. Three new Panel restoration assertions fail before the fix and pass after it with fresh parent native acknowledgement. This changes public JavaScript and therefore requires fresh public consumer/Metro/Release proof; existing CP-59 products are not claimed to contain it.

Private Panel uses NativeFocusView through core Animated and forwards its actual native ref. Explicit initialFocusRef/restoreFocusRef accept View/TextInput refs and reject DOM/focus-shaped objects in compiled private contracts. Entry waits for shown/active presentation, measured layout, native scope acknowledgement and foreground state. A request does not change the observed-focus registry; only the original native focus event acknowledges completion. Explicit null or ineligible targets do not select a fallback. Current scoped Escape respects keyboard guards, original down/up/repeat/cancel/default-capture metadata and the deepest floating layer's exit barrier, preserving repeated onOpenChange(false) requests while an owner holds true.

Return capture precedes overlay admission and retains a real native target/binding/window lease. Actual iOS dismissal and Android hidden-frame barriers precede parent restoration, which waits for the parent's fresh native policy acknowledgement. Removed or replaced target/ref, new native epoch, background/active in one batch, reopened owner and unmount retire old work. No queued callback survives teardown. Synchronous teardown return is admitted only if the surviving parent/target is already valid and ready; full ancestor/teardown restoration remains OPEN where readiness has not returned. Automatic first-descendant traversal, forward/backward containment, physical keyboard/speech and actual native acknowledgements remain mandatory separate capabilities.

SCENE.panel.003 extends the existing maintained Panel fixture with a real opener ref, explicit Close-action entry target, observed-focus copy, close-request count, live light/dark mode, physical-side change and explicit reset. It requests close twice under owner veto, retains the same editor ref and held empty value through theme/side changes, admits closure after the owner changes its guard, then requests return to the surviving opener after native readiness. Existing SCENE.panel.001/.002 retain their composition/routing meaning. Native capture/blur output and installed actions are not inferred from these source-host tests.

## 4. Progress And Meter Delivery

On 2026-10-17 Gabriel requests a persistent component-delivery goal and approves the paired LinearProgress/Meter native contract in the roadmap. The complete canonical component/helper/CSS/root-suite/index audit finds both families unchanged from manifest revision `ddc9f066f67af4742e05e04bd870726141c44129`. The focused web baseline passes 26/26 cases across the two suites in 4.43 seconds. Inherited React Aria range/render/formatting contracts and native role/value types are inspected locally; native implementation and all installed/device proof remain pending at this planning checkpoint. Further XCTest/LLDB startup investigations are excluded from this goal, while RN-TEXT-01 and the original ComboBox installed gate retain their prior outcomes.

Component commit `2b39444` adds LinearProgress and Meter with shared private range rendering and motion handling, plus public prop/ref/render-state types. Required controlled values drive bounded range geometry, accessibility and callback state without a local value mirror. The implementation preserves semantic/default and five existing order colors, independent native style slots, geometry/direction/dimensions, string value labels and host-owned Intl formatting. LinearProgress alone supports unknown progress. Core RN animations stop on replacement/unmount; Reduce Motion stays authoritative over a late initial preference query, with static presentation on unavailable preferences. Palette, pins, toolchain, sealed fixtures and signing are unchanged.

Verification passes 64 focused native component cases, all 26 canonical web baseline cases, two new maintained-scene composition cases and 17 retained NativeShowcase cases. Native package build, public contract types and normal native commit hooks pass. The source suite maps all thirteen assertion categories from each web root suite and adds range, formatting, ownership/ref/accessibility and motion cases; this is capability coverage, not a numeric parity claim. Initial test-authoring errors queried deliberately accessibility-hidden decorations, expected exact binary floating point and relied on an empty-string accessibility matcher; corrected tests inspect the hidden visual host props, use numeric tolerance and directly verify the explicit empty string. Type-case placement/mock overload errors are corrected before the passing checks. One initial planning commit hook used the bundled package manager and aborted before install; rerunning with the existing pinned Node/pnpm PATH passes the normal hooks without dependency changes.

Correction `f7783c2` follows final source review of a pinned-RN platform distinction: Android accepts the ARIA meter enum but its delegate maps it to no accessibility role, suppressing the progressbar fallback. The component uses progressbar compatibility explicitly on Android and retains the meter role on iOS; a targeted platform case verifies the Android binding and bounded value without inventing editability. This remains source evidence, not installed speech proof.

The maintained Progress/Meter scene and curated native reference accompany this source milestone. The documentation build passes all 19 pages and its generated native reference contains the new usage examples. Both source counterparts are available through the public native barrel, taking the catalogue to 18 of 53 source implementations. No installed Android/iOS, measured rendering/motion or physical screen-reader acceptance is claimed. No simulator, XCTest, LLDB, app rebuild, signing or frozen text-fixture mutation occurs. RN-TEXT-01 and the original ComboBox acceptance remain open independently; the next component proposal can proceed from the remaining 35 catalogue rows rather than reopening the lab by default.

The failed observation interleaves the first change, an owner commit and result replacement before the remaining key-callback batch. The passing observation delivers all 17 change callbacks before the owner commit/result replacement. This identifies an ordering difference worth isolating; it does not prove the render or async replacement lost characters, nor reconstruct UIKit application timing.

The exact installed RN 0.86.3 source was inspected and copied with SHA-256 identities into the private packet:

- Fabric RCTTextInputComponentView.mm emits onKeyPress inside textInputShouldChangeText before acceptance/change publication. Default maxLength is int max and the fixture sets no limit. Key callbacks therefore establish proposals at that boundary, not accepted/applied edits.
- RCTBackedTextInputDelegateAdapter.mm calls the proposal delegate, then sets textDidChangeIsComing when it accepts the same replacement. EditingChanged clears that flag and calls textInputDidChange. Its pending-change fallback on end editing is a possible branch, not observed execution in the original failure.
- Fabric textInputDidChange has JS-origin and next-change suppression branches; updateState/setTextAndSelection guard controlled feedback with event-count equality. Neither the original JS trace nor the later post-verdict capture can identify every branch taken during typing.
- TextInput.js rebuilds setLocalRef when mostRecentEventCount changes. useMergeRefs/useRefEffect can clean up and reassign the same instance when that callback changes. This supplies an upstream explanation for the recorded same-instance ref callbacks; those callbacks do not establish a native view remount. No Codon ref/synchronization patch is justified by their presence.

A private observer variant changes only the post-focused-verdict hold so both raw conditions can receive read-only UIKit/KVC snapshots. Its binary SHA-256 is 033de169c329e6697c1f070ea1ee9c9aa4bb233c687d8b2dd6da31e72d620de1. The Release application remains byte-identical; the observer is a distinct condition from prior inspector4a645f3d. These are helper/source checks, not new native input proof.

The next bounded run keeps the same retained 89459775 guest and frozen Release app. Its initial limit is 60 minutes, two owned host-probe attachments, three simulator attachments and two gated raw invocations. Native stack samples locate successive expensive attachment stages: executable scripting-resource/dSYM discovery, BoundsSafety instrumentation-runtime symbol checks, then debugserver-requested symbol lookup through DWARF/Mach-O processing. The private session profile defers symbol/module/script discovery, disables external lookup and the debugger's BoundsSafety discovery plugin, and disables local-variable injection for expressions. These changes are confined to that LLDB process; they neither disable application checks nor prove generally fast attachment. Attachment02 hits its 180-second cap before native metadata or typing. Attachment03 completes attachment and UIKit imports, but its metadata expression fails compilation because Objective-C exceptions are disabled, untyped message sends are rejected and local names collide with injected symbols. Its outer elapsed time is 227.752 s; the field helper's 0.0166 s value measures reuse of an already stopped process, not actual attach latency. Cleanup detaches successfully, with zero typing and zero breakpoint events.

The corrected metadata expression uses runtime ivar lookup, explicit casts and prefixed local names, with no KVC exception handler or target setters. Its complete Objective-C++ wrapper passes an exceptions-disabled simulator-SDK syntax check using a private module cache. That is preparation evidence; live evaluation still has to pass before releasing the unchanged typing call. Prepared auto-continue address breakpoints observe proposal/change/selection/state/controlled-write paths with read-only arguments and counters, capped at 90 seconds/300 events. Native formatter reads must first demonstrate that they do not run the target. This is a separate observer condition whose scheduling can affect the burst; it cannot replace the frozen first/warm comparison.

A separate private preflight defect is isolated: the immediate Transport 1 assertion can run before the controlled owner update. The failed warm preflight's later screenshot/hierarchy already shows Transport 1. Only the private no-editor preflight gains a bounded 15-second exact-label expectation after its single tap; the raw focused assertion and unpaced typing remain unchanged. The rebuilt private observer then passes one actual callback test in 100.66 s with an exported Transport 1 attachment. The application is not rebuilt, and its 67 files remain frozen. This callback pass does not guarantee the next application launch.

The single approved replay completes with xcodebuild exit 65 after 216.76 s: testRaw fails in application launch with Timed out attempting to launch app, before opening the raw case or reaching the release gate. The completed result and failure attachments are exported. No fourth simulator attachment is started, no corrected metadata expression is evaluated live and no typing runs. The controller's attempted focused-result parse therefore has no raw verdict to parse; this is an application-launch failure, not a failed text assertion. No further replay follows.

Final native totals are one host-probe attachment, three simulator attachments, three gated raw invocations, zero typing calls, zero focused text verdicts and zero native breakpoint events. Basic LLDB read/detach is proven, but reliable XCTest startup and live metadata evaluation remain unproven. The prepared probe is not promoted as a validated native mutation tracer. These observations do not establish a unique causal repair, PID authorization, AX readiness or the actual owner callback.

A read-only thread observation finds one private-runner thread in an uninterruptible wait, not a stopped state. A subsequent one-second sample returns exit0 while the runner disappears and produces no stack artifact. That is failed stack proof and a scheduling confound; this run cannot attribute the kill independently of sampling. The earlier unsampled bootstrap kill remains separate evidence. No matching new crash report or narrowly queried host log identifies the killer. Read-only inspection finds an Apple-signed runner and no quarantine attribute; signing/security are not changed. A later memory snapshot shows 8261.50 MiB of swap in use, while top collection is unavailable after a permission failure and capped read. These observations do not prove a unique resource cause. The validation without runner probes has no sampling/thread inspection and fails at launch instead.

Preparation and orchestration errors remain separate from XCTest: the copied seal verifier first rejects its stale parent root before writing, then is corrected within the fresh packet. The single-condition validation script also emits an unused warm-transition/cooldown message and runs a service-list query that reaches its 90-second cap; it creates no warm XCTest invocation. Neither actual XCTest failure reaches its 600/300-second total/silence allowance. Aggregate totals are two boots of the same retained guest, two existing-daemon activations, two XCTest invocations, zero new guests or service/security resets and zero LLDB attachments. Selected methods, actual callback passes, typing calls and focused editor verdicts are zero; acceptance never runs.

Final cleanup restores the exact diagnostic app67, stops all five task guests and closes only identified task Simulator UI. The original priority app67, installed private runner34, cached observer identities, source62/archive/locks/pins/palette and global Developer selection verify unchanged. Font preferences are not written or freshly queried. All 21 inherited manifests/10242 listed files verify as data; sealed helpers are never executed/imported. The fresh native-xctest-control-session-2026-10-17 packet under the existing chat evidence root retains the complete sources, activation/guard records, original failure receipts, sampling limitations, clean validation and cleanup. The initial control connection is quick in these prepared conditions, but native ComboBox acceptance and RN-TEXT-01 remain open at runner launch/test-bundle bootstrap and later AX/callback prerequisites. Another unchanged control-session replay is not the next gate.

## 12. Historical Document Lookup

The 44 former top-level documents in docs/roadmaps are consolidated into this roadmap and evidence companion. Their complete text, exact proposal/receipt scope and former native ledger sections 8.1–8.56 remain available at local Git revision db54a8a. Resolve any historical path with `git show db54a8a:<path>`; this does not require a published branch or tracker. Existing immutable proof-packet sourceRef strings (including DateTimePicker) deliberately retain their former paths and resolve through this lookup. They are provenance, not current task links. Settled public behavior is documented in the curated pages referenced from the roadmap; no registry artifact or CLI contract is changed by removing duplicate planning prose.
